. The third type of criticality is established only for the HYL model and is given as the existence of two simultaneous minimiser (zeroes) of the LDP rate function for a critical parameter
\mu=\mu^{*}
, see Theorem
3.14
. As one of the two densities is strictly smaller than the other one, the difference shall represent the so-called condensate density. However, we do not know if this excess density corresponds to the density of the so-called infinitely long loops.

To investigate this further, we shall find an order parameter. In
[
Gir60
]
, Girardeau suggests an order parameter for the (so-called
generalised
) Bose-Einstein condensation phase transition which has been further studied by
[
Lew86
,
BLP88
]
. Having in mind the density of particles with zero single particle energy in the thermodynamic limit, they first take the finite volume expected density of particles with energy below some cut-off. Then the thermodynamic limit is taken before finally the cut-off goes to zero. In contrast to these momentum-space perspectives, we do not keep track of the particles’ energy. Instead, we partition our gas by loop type, and expect the condensate to occupy loops of diverging length. Therefore we want to evaluate the ‘condensate density’ given by

\Delta\left(\beta,\alpha\right):=\lim_{K\to\infty}\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\left[D-D_{K}\right],\qquad D_{K}\left(x\right):=\sum^{K}_{k=1}kx_{k}\,.

We analyse first the reference measure (ideal gas) and the CMF model in the following theorem.

Theorem 3.16
.

The following statements hold for the reference measure and the CMF model.
For
\beta>0
,
\alpha<0

a\geq 0
, we have
\Delta\left(\beta,\alpha\right)=\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\alpha)=0
, and for
\beta>0
,
\alpha=0
,
a\geq 0

\Delta\left(\beta,0\right)=\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}(\beta,0)=\begin{cases}+\infty&,\mbox{ for }d=1,2\,,\\
0&,\mbox{ for }d\geq 3\,.\end{cases}

Mathematically, the critical behaviour is seen in the thermodynamic limit of distribution function of the empirical particle density (called Kac distribution), the limit of the Kac distribution exists only for densities
\varrho<\varrho_{\rm c}
and is the degenerate distribution

\mathbb{K}(x)=\begin{cases}0&,x<\varrho,\\
1&,x\geq\varrho.\end{cases}

This critical behaviour is also seen in the rate function for the density large deviation principle in Theorem
2.6
. We believe that the unexpected results in Theorem
3.16
are due to this degeneracy.

The next theorem confirms the conjecture that
(\mu/a-\varrho(\alpha))_{+}
represents the condensate density.

Theorem 3.17
.

For all
\beta>0
,
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and
\alpha\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}\left(\beta,\alpha,\mu\right)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\mu}{a}-\varrho(\alpha)\Big)_{+}=\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}\left(H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}-H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}\right)\Big)\left(\xi^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}\right),

where
\xi^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}
is the unique minimiser (zero) of the rate function
I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\alpha,\mu}
. Furthermore,

\lim_{\alpha\uparrow 0}\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\alpha,\mu)=\big(\frac{\mu}{a}-\varrho_{\rm c}(d)\big)_{+}=\begin{cases}0&,\mbox{ for }d=1,2\,,\\
>&,\mbox{ for }d\geq 3,\mu>a\varrho_{\rm c}(d)\,.\end{cases}

The following result shows that the condensate density has a limit for certain regimes of thermodynamic parameter
\beta
and
\mu
and energy parameter
a
and
b
. Explicit expressions for the minimiser and condensate density in the critical regime are not available.

Theorem 3.18
.

For
\beta>0
,
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
,
\alpha\leq 0
, where the derivative is defined,

\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\mu,\alpha)=-\lim_{K\to\infty}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\Big(\inf_{\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)}\left\{I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\alpha,\mu+s}+sD_{K}\right\}-p^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}\left(\beta,\alpha,\mu+s,\right)\Big)\Big|_{s=0}.

(3.20)

In particular, if the infimum is achieved by
\xi\left(s\right)\in C^{1}\left(\left(-\epsilon,\epsilon\right):\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)\right)
for some
\epsilon>0
, then

\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}\left(\beta,\alpha,\mu\right)=\frac{a}{a-b}\Big(\frac{\mu}{a}-D\left(\xi\left(0\right)\right)\Big)_{+}=\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}\left(H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}-H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}\right)\Big)\left(\xi\left(0\right)\right).

(3.21)

Remark 3.19

(
Conclusion HYL model
)
.

The BEC phase transition for the HYL model is established as follows. Proposition
establishes a subcritical regime for which the pressure is smooth and its derivative gives the particle density with no condensation.
Depending on the density
D(\xi(0))
, for large enough
\mu
the particle density in loops of unbounded length is not vanishing, see Theorem
3.18
. In Figure
5
we can identify the regime
\mu\geq\mu_{\rm p}
when the density of the zero of the rate function is decreasing with increasing
\mu
such that the excess density is carried by loops of unbounded length. According to our Theorem
3.14
at criticality
\mu=\mu^{*}
we have two zeroes
\xi^{1}(\mu^{*})
and
\xi^{2}(\mu^{*})
of the rate function with
D(\xi^{1}(\mu^{*}))>\mu^{*}/a>D(\xi^{2}(\mu^{*}))
. Using (
3.21
) in Theorem
3.18
, we can see that
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\alpha,\mu^{*})=\frac{a}{a-b}\big(\frac{\mu^{*}}{a}-D(\xi^{2}(\mu^{*}))\big)_{+}>0
but we are unable to establish that the difference of the densities of the two simultaneous minimiser equals
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}(\beta,\alpha,\mu^{*})
.

Finally, if we choose
a=2b
in (
3.21
), we can recover the results in
[
Lew86
]
and
[
BLP88
]
.
It shows in fact, that for increasing values of the coupling parameter
a
, the condensate density decreases. On the other hand, if the parameter for the counter energy term,
b
, is approaching
a
, the condensate density increases. This is due to the fact that with large counter terms the system distributes the physical particles in as few as possible different cycles lengths. To accommodate the particle density, the only way is to put them in infinitely long cycles. Our analysis actually shows that the BEC phase transition for HYL is more complex and requires further detailed study. For example, at criticality
\mu=\mu^{*}
one would like to study concentration of the underlying measure around the two distant rate function zeroes.

\diamond

4.
Proof of the Large deviations principles

This section contains the proofs for all large deviation principles. We use the large deviation principle of the reference measure in Appendix
B
as a stepping stone towards arriving at LDPs for our models. Section
4.1
and Section
4.2
give the proofs for both mean-field models, i.e, CMF model and PMF model respectively, and Section
4.3
the proof for the HYL model.

4.1.
Proof of Theorem
2.1
- Cycle Mean Field LDP

We are going to apply Varadahn’s Lemma in
[
DZ09
, Theorem 4.3.1]
. To show continuity of
H^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}}
, let us show sequential continuity. This implies continuity since
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
is a metric space. Let
x^{(n)}\rightarrow x
be a convergent sequence in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
, so
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\lvert x^{(n)}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert=0
.
Let
S(x):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}x_{k}
. Then

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\lvert S(x^{(n)})-S(x)\rvert=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\big|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}x^{(n)}_{k}-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}x_{k}\big|\leq\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\lvert x^{(n)}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert=0.

Hence
S
is continuous. We can then write the Hamiltonian as the composition of continuous functions
H^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}}=T\circ S
, where
T\colon\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
x\rightarrow\frac{a}{2}x^{2}
.
We can now simply apply Varadhan’s Lemma. The lower bound

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}}\big]\geq\sup_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{-H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}(x)-I_{\alpha}(x)\big\}

(4.1)

follows easily with
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.4]
. For the corresponding upper bound we simply note that the tail-condition in
[
DZ09
, Theorem 4.3.1]
holds due to
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}(x)\geq 0
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Therefore, with
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
we obtain the corresponding upper bound

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}}\big]\leq\sup_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{-H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{855497}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647441}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle CMF$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{647440}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle CMF$}}}}}}})}(x)-I_{\alpha}(x)\big\}.

(4.2)

We conclude with the statement in Theorem
2.1
by combining the lower bound (
4.1
) and the upper bound (
4.2
).∎

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.2
- Particle Mean Field LDP

To prove the large deviation principle for
\nu_{N,\mu,\alpha}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}
one would simply use Varadhan’s Lemma. However, the first term of the Hamiltonian
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
for
\mu>0
is not lower semicontinuous whereas the second term is only lower semicontinuous. Using
[
GZ93
]
, one would arrive at lower and upper bounds for
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}[{\rm e}^{-\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert\beta H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}]
using the upper and the lower semicontinuous regularisation of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
, respectively. Unfortunately, the upper semicontinuous regularisation of the Hamiltonian equals infinity, and thus it does not provide a lower bound for the large deviation principle. Our strategy is therefore twofold. For the large deviation upper bound we use the lower semicontinuous regularisation in conjunction with the corresponding bound in Varadhan’s Lemma. We obtain the corresponding large deviation lower bound by conditioning that the empirical cycle count is supported on a finite-dimensional subspace. On this event we can replace our measure by the corresponding measure with finite dimensional mark space. On this subspace the Hamiltonian is in fact continuous and thus application of Varadhan’s Lemma provides a lower bound. To remove the cutoff parameter we will construct finite-dimensional sequences approximating the infimum of the corresponding lower bound.
We start with a couple of observations.

Lemma 4.1
.

For all
\mu>0
, the lower semicontinuous regularisation of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
is given as

H^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)-\frac{1}{2a}\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)_{+}^{2}=\begin{cases}-\mu D(x)+\frac{a}{2}D(x)^{2}&,D(x)\geq\frac{\mu}{a},\\
-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2a}&,D(x)<\frac{\mu}{a},\end{cases}\quad x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),

(4.3)

whereas for all
\mu\leq 0
,
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}\equiv H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
.

Proof.
Suppose
h
is lower semicontinuous and
h\left(x\right)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}\left(x\right)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
. Let
x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\to x
in
\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
. Then
h\left(x\right)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}h\left(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}\left(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)
. Let
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}=x_{k}+\frac{1}{na}\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)_{+}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{n=k\}
, so this inequality implies that
h\left(x\right)\leq H^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}\left(x\right)
.

Note that
H^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}\left(x\right)=g\circ D\left(x\right)
, where
g\colon D\mapsto-\mu D+\frac{a}{2}D^{2}-\frac{1}{2a}\left(\mu-aD\right)^{2}_{+}
for
0\leq D<+\infty
and
+\infty\mapsto+\infty
. Since
g
is continuous and non-decreasing (see Figure
1
), and
D\left(x\right)
is lower semicontinuous, the composition is also lower semicontinuous.
∎

Proposition 4.2

(
Upper bound PMF model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>0
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha,\mu}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\big]\leq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\}\,.

(4.4)

Proof.
The statement follows easily with the upper bound estimate in Varadhan’s Lemma given in
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
using the inequality
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, the lower semicontinuity of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}
, and the fact that
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\geq-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2a}
. The later estimate provides the tail-condition necessary to apply
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
.
∎

Proposition 4.3

(
Lower bound PMF model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>0
,

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha,\mu}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\big]\geq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\}\,.

(4.5)

Proof.
The strategy for proving the lower bound is to first introduce a cut-off parameter as done in
[
ACK11
]
, that is, we change the measure to obtain a finite-dimensional problem which gives continuity of the Hamiltonian and thus a large deviation lower bound for the finite-dimensional space. The final step is then to remove the cut-off parameter. As our Hamiltonian is not positive, removing of the cut-off is not as straightforward as in
[
ACK11
]
. We thus need to construct a sequence for the finite dimensional spaces which allows for energy estimates and at the same time gives convergence towards the lower semicontinuous regularisation. The last step is crucial as a lower bound can be obtained via the upper semicontinuous regularisation which in our case is identical to infinity.

Step 1: Restriction of the mark space.
We will approximate the infinite-dimensional space
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
by finite-dimensional spaces. Pick some
K\in\ N
, and the corresponding measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}
which is isomorphic to
\pi_{K}(\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))
with
\pi_{K}\colon\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\to\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K},x\mapsto(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})
, is denoted

\nu_{N,\alpha}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}=\nu_{N,\alpha}\circ\pi_{K}^{-1}\,.

We obtain a large deviation principle for the cut-off version in the following.

Lemma 4.4
.

For given
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\leq 0
, the sequence
(\nu_{N,\alpha}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies a LDP on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}
with rate
\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert
and rate function

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)=\sum^{K}_{k=1}\Big[\frac{x_{k}}{\beta}\Big(\log\frac{x_{k}}{q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}-1\Big)+\frac{q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}{\beta}\Big]\,.

(4.6)

Proof.
Since the projection
\pi_{K}
is continuous, we can apply the contraction principle to obtain a variational form of the rate function

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)=\inf_{\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\colon\pi_{K}(\widetilde{x})=x}I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x})\,,

where
I_{\alpha}
is the rate function for
\nu_{N,\alpha}
in Proposition
B.1
. We conclude using that projection is continuous.
∎

Step 2: Lower bound.

We obtain a lower bound

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\big]\geq\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}\}\big]\,,

where we identified
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}
with the corresponding subspace in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. On that event we can replace
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
by

H_{\mu,K}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(x)=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx_{k}\Big)^{2}\,,

and
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}
by
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}}
- up to a factor of
\nu_{N,\alpha}\left(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}\right)=\exp\left(-\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert\sum^{\infty}_{k=K+1}q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}\right)
. The finite-dimensional approximation
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}
is in fact continuous, and thus we obtain a large deviation lower bound using Lemma
4.4
and Varadhan’s Lemma, see
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.4]
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\big]\geq-\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}}\big\{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}(x)\big\}-\sum^{\infty}_{k=K+1}q_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}\,.

(4.7)

Step 3: Removing the cut-off parameter.
We are left to remove the cut-off by taking
K\to\infty
and to prove that the
K\to\infty
limit of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}
is replaced by the lower semicontinuous regularisation of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
. The sum vanishes in the
K\to\infty
limit because it converges.

Lemma 4.5
.

\limsup_{K\to\infty}\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{K}}\big\{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}(x)\big\}\leq\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\}\,.

(4.8)

Proof.
Fix
\widetilde{x}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
satisfying
I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x})+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(\widetilde{x})<\infty
. For
K\in\mathbb{N}
, consider
x^{K}=\pi_{K}(\widetilde{x})
. By (
4.6
), we have
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x^{K})\leq I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x})
. We shall replace
x^{K}
by
\widehat{x}^{K}
, defined as

\widehat{x}^{K}_{k}=\begin{cases}x^{K}_{k},&,k=1,\ldots,K-1,\\
x^{K}_{K}+\frac{1}{K}\left(\frac{\mu}{a}-D(\widetilde{x})\right)_{+},&,k=K.\end{cases}

Clearly,
\lVert x^{K}-\widehat{x}^{K}\rVert_{\ell_{1}}\to 0
as
K\to\infty
, and
x^{K}\to\widetilde{x}
as
K\to\infty
. Furthermore,

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(\widehat{x}^{K})\leq I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x^{K})+\lvert I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(\widehat{x}^{K})-I^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({K}})}}_{\alpha}(x^{K})\rvert\leq I_{\alpha}(\widetilde{x})+\text{O}(\frac{1}{K}\log K)\,.

We turn to the energy term which needs extra care as the Hamiltonian is not positive. Observe that

D(\widehat{x}^{K})=\begin{cases}\frac{\mu}{a}+D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})&,D(\widehat{x})<\frac{\mu}{a}\,,\\
D(x^{K})&,D(\widehat{x})\geq\frac{\mu}{a}\,.\end{cases}

Assume that
D(\widetilde{x})\geq\frac{\mu}{a}
, so

H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}(\widehat{x}^{K})=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx^{K}_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}kx^{K}_{k}\Big)^{2}\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(\widetilde{x})\,.

In the other case,
D(\widetilde{x})<\frac{\mu}{a}
, for every
\varepsilon>0
choose
K
sufficiently large such that
\lvert D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})\rvert<\varepsilon
, and estimate

\displaystyle H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,K}(\widehat{x}^{K})

\displaystyle=-\mu\Big(\frac{\mu}{a}+D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})\Big)+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\frac{\mu}{a}+D(x^{K})-D(\widetilde{x})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(\widetilde{x})+2\mu\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}\frac{a}{2}

to conclude with (
4.8
).
∎

We finally combine Proposition
4.4
and Proposition
4.3
to finish the proof for Theorem
2.2
.

∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
2.4

This section proves Theorem
2.4
using techniques which are similar to the ones in the proof in Section
4.2
. However, there are significant differences to address due to the fact that the Hamiltonian
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
has positive and negative contributions. We rewrite the Hamiltonian in two equivalent ways for any
a\geq b>0
and
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}+\frac{(a-b)}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}\Big)^{2}+\frac{b}{2}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j,k=1}{j\not= k}}^{\infty}jkx_{j}x_{k}

(4.9)

\displaystyle=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}\Big)^{2}-\frac{b}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}.

(4.10)

Note that that the right hand side in (
4.9
) is the sum of a PMF Hamiltonian with interaction strength
(a-b)
and a lower semicontinuous and non-negative term. On the other hand, (
4.10
) expresses
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}
as the sum of a PMF Hamiltonian, and an upper semicontinuous and non-positive term. Let us introduce the following notations

\displaystyle H_{\mu,a}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}(x)=-\mu\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}+\frac{a}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kx_{k}\Big)^{2},\quad a>0,

\displaystyle H_{+}(x)=\frac{b}{2}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j,k=1}{j\not= k}}^{\infty}jkx_{j}x_{k},\qquad H_{-}(x)=-\frac{b}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}.

Thus
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}=H_{\mu,a}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}+H_{-}=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a-b}+H_{+}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

For
b>0
,
H_{-}
is upper semicontinuous and
H_{+}
is lower semicontinuous on
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

Proof.
We shall show that
\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{j,k=1}{j\not= k}}^{\infty}jkx_{j}x_{k}
and
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}
are both lower semicontinuous on
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Suppose
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\to x
in
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Clearly,
\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert\leq\lVert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}-x\rVert_{\ell_{1}}\quad\mbox{ for all }k\in\mathbb{N}
. Furthermore, due to the
\ell_{1}
-convergence and
\ell_{1}\subset\ell_{\infty}
, the term
\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert
is bounded in both
k
and
n
. Hence

\lvert(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})^{2}-x_{k}^{2}\rvert=\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}+x_{k}\rvert\to 0\;\mbox{ as }n\to\infty.

Applying Fatou’s Lemma here proves that
H_{-}
is upper semicontinuous. Similarly, for all
(j,k)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
,

\lvert x_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}x_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}-x_{j}x_{k}\rvert\leq\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{j}\rvert\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-x_{k}\rvert+\lvert x_{k}\rvert\lvert x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{j}-x_{j}\rvert\to 0\;\mbox{ as }n\to\infty.

This convergence in conjunction with Fatou’s Lemma shows that
H_{+}
is lower semicontinuous.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

For
a>b
, the
\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
lower semicontinuous regularisation of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
is given by

H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)-\frac{\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)_{+}^{2}}{2(a-b)},\quad x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}).

(4.11)

Proof.
Denote the right hand side of (
4.11
) by
h
. Clearly,
h(x)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)
and
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\leq h(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)+H_{+}(x)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. We need to show that
h
is the greatest lower semicontinuous function less or equal to
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}
.

Suppose that
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)=\infty
. Then since
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)=\infty
, we have
h(x)=\infty
. Suppose now that
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
with
D(x)<\infty
. For any sequence
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
x_{n}\to x
as
n\to\infty
there exists
(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
x_{n}=x+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}
,
\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, and
x+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. Furthermore,
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left(D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-D(x)\right)\geq 0
and thus
\liminf_{n\to\infty}D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\geq 0
. We show that
h
is lower semicontinuous by proving that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left(h(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-h(x)\right)=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{-\mu\left(D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-D(x)\right)+\frac{a}{2}\left(D(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})^{2}-D(x)^{2}\right)\\
-\frac{b}{2}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}\left((x_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})^{2}-x_{k}^{2}\right)\Big)+\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\Big(\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)_{+}^{2}-\left(\mu-aD(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)_{+}^{2}\Big)\Big\}\geq 0.

(4.12)

If
D\left(\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)\to+\infty
, then
\liminf_{n\to\infty}h\left(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)=+\infty
, so we suppose that
D\left(\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)
is finite and bounded.

We write
\varepsilon_{k}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}=\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}-\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}
with
\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k},\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\geq 0
. Clearly,
\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\leq x_{k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
We shall show both

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}

\displaystyle=0,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}(x_{k}+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}+\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left(D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}.

(4.14)

As
D(x)<\infty
, for all
C>0
there exits
K_{C}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
kx_{k}<C
for all
k>K_{C}
. Therefore we have
\sum^{\infty}_{k=K_{C}+1}k^{2}x_{k}\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}_{k}<C\sum^{\infty}_{k=K_{C}+1}k\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}_{k}\leq CD\left(\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)
. Then since
\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum^{K_{C}}_{k=1}k^{2}x_{k}\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}_{k}=0
, and
D\left(\varepsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)
is bounded, we can choose
C
arbitrarily small to get (
4.13
). To obtain (
4.14
) we just expand

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}(x_{k}+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}x_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}(x_{k}-\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k})\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}+\left(D(\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}.

(4.15)

The middle term vanishes due to (
4.13
). To show that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left(D(\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left(D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2},

(4.16)

note that
D(x)<\infty
implies that
D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})-D(\varepsilon^{+{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})=D(\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\leq D(x)<\infty
. Hence, for any
\delta
there exists
K\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sum_{k=K+1}^{\infty}k\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}\leq\sum_{k=K+1}^{\infty}kx_{k}<\frac{\delta}{2}
. On the other hand, for this
\delta
there exists a
n(K)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{k=1}^{K}k\varepsilon^{-{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}<\frac{\delta}{2},\quad\mbox{ for all }n\geq n(K),

(4.17)

thus showing (
4.14
). We continue with

\mbox{ r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc} }\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\left(\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)_{+}^{2}-\left(\mu-aD(x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)_{+}^{2}\right)\\
-(\mu-aD(x))D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+\frac{a-b}{2}\left(D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}\Big\}.

(4.18)

Recall that
\liminf_{n\to\infty}D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\geq 0
, and thus we know that
(\mu-aD(x))<0
implies that eventually
(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}))<0
and
(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+bD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}))<0
.
Suppose that
\mu/a<D(x)
. Then

\mbox{r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc2} }=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{-\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+\frac{a-b}{2}D(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})^{2}\Big\}\geq 0.

(4.19)

Suppose
\mu/a\geq D(x)
and
\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\leq 0
. Then

\mbox{ r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc2} }\geq\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left\{\left(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+bD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}\right\}\geq 0,

(4.20)

and likewise for
\mu/a\geq D(x)
and
\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})>0
,

\mbox{r.h.s. of \eqref{hlsc2} }\geq\frac{1}{2(a-b)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\Big\{\left(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})+bD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}\\
-\left(\mu-aD(x)-aD(\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}})\right)^{2}\Big\}\geq 0.

(4.21)

We have established (
4.12
) and thus the lower semicontinuity of
h
. Suppose
f
is lower semicontinuous and
f\left(x\right)\leq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}\left(x\right)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
. Let
x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\to x
in
\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
. Then
f\left(x\right)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}f\left(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}\left(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}\right)
. Let
x^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}=x+\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}
with

\varepsilon^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}_{k}={\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{k=n\}\frac{\left(\mu-aD(x)\right)_{+}}{n(a-b)},

(4.22)

so this inequality implies that
f\left(x\right)\leq h\left(x\right)
.
∎

Proposition 4.8

(
Upper bound HYL model
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>b\geq 0
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\leq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\}.

(4.23)

Proof.
The statement follows easily with the upper bound estimate in Varadhan’s Lemma given in
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
using the inequality
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\geq H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,(a-b),\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)
for all
x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, the lower semicontinuity of
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}
, and the fact that
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\geq-\frac{\left(\mu\right)^{2}}{2(a-b)}
. The latter estimate provides the tail-condition necessary to apply
[
DZ09
, Lemma 4.3.6]
.
∎

For the lower bound we are using the lower bound (
4.5
) for the PMF model and
H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a}+H_{-}
with
H_{-}
being upper semicontinuous.

Proposition 4.9

(
Lower bound HYLmodel
)
.

For all
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\alpha\leq 0
, and
a>b\geq 0
,

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\Big]\geq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\}.

(4.24)

Proof.
Using

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu}}\big]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha,\mu}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H_{-}}\big]\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a}}\big]

in conjunction with the LDP in Theorem
2.2
and in particular the lower bound (
4.5
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert}\log\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha,\mu}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H_{-}}\big]\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N,\alpha}}\big[{\rm e}^{-\beta\lvert\Lambda_{N}\rvert H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a}}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\alpha,\mu}(x)+H_{-}(x)\big\}-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}\big\}

\displaystyle=-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)+\beta H_{-}(x)\big\}=-\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\},

where the last equality follows from Lemma
4.10
below.

∎

Lemma 4.10
.

\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)+H_{-}(x)\big\}=\inf_{x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big\{I_{\alpha}(x)+H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\big\}\,.

Proof.
The infimum of any function on an open set is equal to the infimum of its lower semicontinuous regularisation over the same set. Note that the
\ell_{1}
topology restricted to
\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
has
\ell_{1}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
as open. We thus need to show that

\big(H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{646131}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle{PMF}$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,a,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}+H_{-}\big)_{\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)=H^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{792963}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585927}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle HYL$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{585928}{167117}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle HYL$}}}}}}})}_{\mu,\rm\tiny l.s.c.}(x)\quad\mbox{ for all }x\in\ell_{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}).

(4.25)

Note that
